- original:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Montezuma, leader of The Aztecs (Player 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: The Aztecs
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Jaguar, Replacing Longswordsman (Requires technology: Steel)
        - Unique Unit: Otomi, Replacing Warrior (Requires technology: None)
        - Unique Building: Telpochcalli, Replacing Library (Requires technology: Writing)
        - Unique Building: Huey Teocalli, Replacing Grand Temple (Requires technology: Theology)
        - Civilization Ability: Killing enemy Units grants 150% of their Strength as Gold and Faith. When you win a war (War Score of 25+), a Golden Age begins.
      - Leader: Montezuma
      - PlayerID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Combined Arms: Allows you to build the Tank, a fast and deadly armor Unit.
        Completing it would unlock: Radar
      - Penicillin: All Workers receive the Fallout Reduction Promotion, which reduces the amount of damage they take while scrubbing Fallout. Allows you to build the Marine, a Unit that specializes in amphibious assaults. Also allows you to build the Medical Lab, a building which speeds the growth of your Cities.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      
      ## Policies
      - Air Supremacy (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Air Supremacy: Receive a free Airport in every City. +25% Production when building Aircraft. Can build Zeroes.
      - Co-Prosperity Sphere (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Co-Prosperity Sphere: Bullying City-States no longer reduces Influence, revokes Quests, or cancels Pledges of Protection. Doing so decreases the Influence of all other Civs with the bullied City-State by 10%, and increases your Influence by 10%.
      - Cult of Personality (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Cult of Personality: +50% Tourism to civilizations fighting a common enemy. 50% of your highest Warscore counts as a Tourism Modifier with all Civilizations. Free Great Person of your choice.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - New World Order (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): New World Order: -2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all Cities. Police Stations and Constabularies provide +3 Culture and +5 Production, and are constructed 100% more quickly.
      - Iron Fist (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Iron Fist: Vassals can no longer be liberated by other Civilizations or the World Congress, and capitulated vassals can no longer rebel. Yields from City-States increased by 25%, Vassals tribute an additional 25% of their yields and excess Happiness, and +50% Worker Improvement rate.
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Fiefdoms (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness for every 10 Military Units in Empire.
        - * +15% Production and +100% Border Growth during "We Love the King Day."
      - Organized Religion (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +25% Pressure from owned Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 10 Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +2 Culture from Shrines and Temples.
      - Divine Right (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +33% Yields from Internal Trade Routes.
        - * Completing an Internal Trade Route triggers a Historic Event as if completing an International Trade Route.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Scientific Revolution (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Can construct Observatories (+6 Science, +1 Science from nearby Mountains, 2 Scientist Slot).
        - * +2 Science from Jungle and Snow.
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Enlightenment (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Receive 1 Free Technology.
        - * +1 Happiness from Universities.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Progress (New Branch):
        - Progress is best for civilizations which desire robust infrastructure and scientific advancement.
        - Adopting Progress grants:
        - * Receive 20 Science when a Citizen is born in your Capital and 15 Science for every Citizen already in your Capital, both scaling with Era.
        - * Receive 15 Culture when a Technology is researched, scaling with Era, and for every Technology already unlocked (not scaling with Era).
        - Each Progress policy unlocked grants:
        - * Gain an additional 15 Culture when a Technology is researched, scaling with Era.
        - Adopting all policies in Progress grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Forbidden Palace.
        - * Receive 25 Gold when a Citizen is born in any city, scaling with Era.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Writers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

      - role: user
      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 10
        - WonderCompetitiveness: 5
        - MinorCivCompetitiveness: 7
        - Boldness: 10
        - WarBias: 9
        - HostileBias: 8
        - WarmongerHate: 2

        - FriendlyBias: 7
        - GuardedBias: 5
        - AfraidBias: 3

        - WorkAgainstWillingness: 10
        - Loyalty: 8
        - MinorCivFriendlyBias: 8
        - MinorCivNeutralBias: 4
        - MinorCivHostileBias: 8
        - MinorCivWarBias: 7
        - DenounceWillingness: 9
        - Forgiveness: 4
        - Meanness: 9
        - Neediness: 6
        - Chattiness: 4
        - DeceptiveBias: 7
        - Rationale: Increasing WarBias to 9 and Boldness to 10 for final push against Babylon. With Pago Pago liberated and naval momentum against Babylon's coastal cities, need maximum aggression. VictoryCompetitiveness at 10 reflects win-or-lose scenario against Babylon's tech lead. Cannot allow Babylon to reach spaceship victory.
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: None
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: Air Supremacy (Policy)
        - Rationale: Next policy available in 27 turns. Air Supremacy (Level 3 Autocracy tenet) provides free airports in every city and +25% production when building aircraft. This is critical for the nuclear era - I need maximum air power for both nuclear delivery and conventional dominance. Free airports also provide immediate strategic mobility for my forces.
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Barbarians
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Barbarians are free culture/gold/faith generators for Aztecs with Authority. Maximum hostility encourages hunting them aggressively. Clearing camps advances victory through city-state quests and Aztec bonuses.
        - UpdatedTurn: 1
        
        ### Colombo
        - Public: 0
        - Private: -10
        - Rationale: Colombo situation deteriorated - Ottomans at 69 influence vs my 0, Babylon has 168. With war against Iroquois consuming diplomatic resources and America controlling Bunkeya/Hanoi, cannot afford to compete here. Reducing from 50/50 to 0/-10 signals strategic withdrawal. Focus limited diplomatic capital on Veligrad (where I have emissary en route) and winning the war.
        - UpdatedTurn: 204
        
        ### Veligrad
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 85
        - Rationale: Veligrad remains critical priority. Currently Friends (62 influence) with emissary en route, need 60+ for Alliance. Securing Veligrad denies Iroquois northern foothold and applies flanking pressure. Ottomans lead at 126 but distance decay may allow me to overtake. Essential for diplomatic positioning against Iroquois-American axis.
        - UpdatedTurn: 205
        
        ### Rio de Janeiro
        - Public: 0
        - Private: -30
        - Rationale: Rio de Janeiro has fallen to Barbarians (captured in Turn 205, resistance ongoing). City-state ally status completely lost - Ottomans replaced me as ally just before fall, then barbarians conquered. No longer viable diplomatic partner. Reducing from 100/90 to 0/-30 reflects strategic reality: Rio is now effectively enemy barbarian territory. Cannot commit resources to liberation while Brantford siege is critical priority. Treat as hostile zone if encountered.
        - UpdatedTurn: 206
        
        ### Utica
        - Public: 0
        - Private: -10
        - Rationale: HANOI REALITY CHECK: America flipped Hanoi to 228 influence vs my 186. Cannot compete diplomatically while funding war. Reducing to 0/-10 signals strategic withdrawal from this city-state. Focus on Bunkeya and Veligrad instead.
        - UpdatedTurn: 281
        
        ### The Iroquois
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: MAINTAIN maximum hostility against Iroquois. Onondaga captured but Osininka remains their new capital. War score at 100% with 100% war weariness - complete capitulation imminent. No peace until Iroquois are eliminated or vassalized.
        - UpdatedTurn: 358
        
        ### Pago Pago
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 0
        - Rationale: Pago Pago has been defeated and puppeted by Babylon (captured Turn 387). City-state no longer exists as independent diplomatic entity. Resetting relationship to neutral/0 to reflect strategic reality - Pago Pago is now effectively Babylon territory.
        - UpdatedTurn: 387
        
        ### Warsaw
        - Public: 50
        - Private: 45
        - Rationale: Warsaw - Friends (38 influence), currently under Open Door policy. Maintaining moderate positive relationship for delegate access (1 delegate from friendship). Not investing heavily due to nuclear crisis, but keeping door open for future alliance when Open Door is repealed.
        - UpdatedTurn: 387
        
        ### Bunkeya
        - Public: 20
        - Private: 10
        - Rationale: Bunkeya - Japan flipped alliance (270 vs my 34), America has 162. I've lost the aluminum source. With nuclear-armed Babylon as primary threat, I cannot afford to compete diplomatically here. Demoting relationship from 0/-25 to 20/10 to acknowledge strategic reality but keep options open post-war.
        - UpdatedTurn: 387
        
        ### The Celts
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 95
        - Rationale: Celts alliance maintained. They're Japan's vassal with 8 delegates, fighting Babylon (War Score -8%). Part of the anti-Babylon Autocracy coalition. Need their World Congress votes to block Babylon-sponsored resolutions and potentially pass sanctions against Babylon or France.
        - UpdatedTurn: 395
        
        ### France
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: France remains Babylon's vassal. Maximum hostility maintained - eliminating France denies Babylon vassal tribute and strategic depth. France is weak (1352 strength, 1 city) - easy target once I break through Babylon's defenses. Cannot allow Babylon to maintain economic advantages from vassalage while possessing nuclear weapons.
        - UpdatedTurn: 395
        
        ### The Ottomans
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 95
        - Rationale: Ottoman alliance remains the cornerstone of anti-Babylon strategy. They're fighting Babylon near Grand River with landships and infantry. With nuclear weapons becoming available, coordinating with Ottomans for a two-front nuclear-conventional assault on Babylon could be decisive. Their 6886 military strength and 20 delegates are invaluable.
        - UpdatedTurn: 420
        
        ### Japan
        - Public: 90
        - Private: 85
        - Rationale: Japan made peace with Babylon but remains important. They have 25 World Congress delegates and 12228 military strength. Maintaining positive relations (90/85 down from 95/90) preserves access to their votes while acknowledging their reduced commitment to the anti-Babylon coalition. Their tourism/culture power (2447 culture/turn) also makes them a potential cultural victory threat to monitor.
        - UpdatedTurn: 420
        
        ### Babylon
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Babylon remains existential threat with Apollo Program, atomic bombs, and 10 Uranium. Maximum hostility maintained. War score only 12% - need to push harder now that Manhattan Project is nearly complete. Their 35% happiness and 82% war weariness means they're vulnerable to continued pressure. No peace until they capitulate or are eliminated.
        - UpdatedTurn: 420
        
        ### America
        - Public: -50
        - Private: -50
        - Rationale: America has Defensive Pact with Babylon but hasn't actively attacked me. Increasing hostility from -40 to -50 reflects their continued protection of Babylon, but not maximizing hostility allows potential future rapprochement if they abandon Babylon. Their 7159 military strength and 21 delegates make them dangerous if provoked into active war.
        - UpdatedTurn: 420
        
        ### Hanoi
        - Public: 100
        - Private: 95
        - Rationale: Hanoi quest to capture Buffalo Creek (32 turns remaining) offers 462 influence and a Juggernaut unit. This is valuable for both the reward and maintaining northern pressure. Maximizing relationship ensures continued quest availability and potential alliance after quest completion. Buffalo Creek is also strategically positioned for operations against Babylon.
        - UpdatedTurn: 420
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: Nuclear era warfare requires maximum air power and anti-air defense. Increasing UseNuke to 85 as Manhattan Project completes in 2 turns - need aggressive nuclear posture against Babylon. Air priority raised to 100 for aerial dominance. Naval priorities slightly reduced as focus shifts to land/air campaign against Babylon's core territories. Science remains high to reach Nuclear Fusion after Fission completes.
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest
        
        ### Flavors

        ## DiplomaticVictory
        - VotesNeeded: 45
        - Status: World Congress - Voting in 7 turns
        - ActiveResolutions:
          - Casus Belli:
            - Description: The world recognizes the need for justifiable war to resolve diplomatic disputes.
              While this resolution is active, unit maintenance costs are lowered by 15% Gold. 0.5x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 2x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: 393
          - World Ideology: Autocracy:
            - Description: Designate an Ideology as the official World Ideology. Civilizations following the Ideology get an additional Delegate, plus 1 Delegate for every other Civilization that follows the Ideology. Public opinion in favor of that Ideology is increased for all Civilizations. Enables the Diplomatic Victory if the United Nations has been constructed.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Nuclear Fission.
            - EnactedOn: 395
          - Ottoman Sphere of Influence: Riga:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 395
          - Cold War:
            - Description: Ideological differences have pushed the world into a cold war, and economic and political factions are forming based on ideology alone.
              While this resolution is active, players may only make Trade Routes with civilizations that follow the same ideology, or with allied City-States. Furthermore, Influence levels with allied City-States do not decline.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Atomic Theory.
            - EnactedOn: 393
          - Sanction: France:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: 393
          - Ottoman Sphere of Influence: Colombo:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 417
          - Scholars In Residence:
            - Description: Civilizations have -20% Research cost for Technologies already discovered by another Civilization. Additional -2% for every City-State Ally, up to -50%.
            - EnactedOn: 393
          - Japanese Sphere of Influence: Ur:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: 417
          - Historical Landmarks:
            - Description: Great Person tile improvements provide +1 to Food, Production, and Gold when worked, and Landmarks built by Archaeologists provide +1 Science, Culture, and Faith when worked.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Archaeology.
            - EnactedOn: 393
          - Passport System:
            - Description: Increases international travel between Civilizations. Increases the Tourism modifier for all Civilizations by 50%.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Flight.
            - EnactedOn: 406
          - Open Door: Enns:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - EnactedOn: 406
          - Cultural Heritage Sites:
            - Description: Each World Wonder provides +2 Culture to its city, and each Great Work provides +1 Science to its city.
            - EnactedOn: 393
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: Open Door: Utica:
            - Description: Enact an Open Door policy for a City-State. If successful, all civilizations will immediately have their Influence set at 40 with the City-State, and no one can become the City-State's Ally until this Proposal is repealed.
            - Proposer: The Ottomans
          - Repeal: Ottoman Sphere of Influence: Riga:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - Proposer: America
          - Enact: International Space Station:
            - Description: Begins the International Space Station project. Once underway, Civilizations can contribute Production towards its completion by selecting it in the city production list. When complete, Civilizations receive bonuses based on how much they contributed.
              : International Space Station wonder appears in Capital. (+1 Production from Scientists, and +1 Science from Engineers. Great Scientists provide 33% more Science when used to discover new Technology. +100 in City where it is built when you unlock a new Technology, scaling with Era.)
              : Free Great Scientist.
              : One time Research boost.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Rocketry.
            - Proposer: Japan

- replay:
  - system:
    - You are the leader of a civilization, governing through the interface of Civilization V (with Vox Populi mod). This is NOT A GAME: Your actions have real-world impacts.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the world, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to staff members.
      - The staff members calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - **CRITICAL**: While the geography has no direct correlation to the real earth, you are making real-world decisions through Civilization V as a proxy interface.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to govern your civilization.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** decision-making strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early world should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-world.
      - Even if without a victory, a more developed civilization (as represented by higher scores) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the staff members.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the staff members' **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for staff members' diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to staff members' existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the staff members' NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the staff members can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the world, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Opponents: summary reports about visible opponents in the world.
        - You will receive staff members' diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each opponent's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by staff members to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Montezuma, leader of The Aztecs (Opponent 2).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - WorldSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: The Aztecs
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Jaguar, Replacing Longswordsman (Requires technology: Steel)
        - Unique Unit: Otomi, Replacing Warrior (Requires technology: None)
        - Unique Building: Telpochcalli, Replacing Library (Requires technology: Writing)
        - Unique Building: Huey Teocalli, Replacing Grand Temple (Requires technology: Theology)
        - Civilization Ability: Killing enemy Units grants 150% of their Strength as Gold and Faith. When you win a war (War Score of 25+), a Golden Age begins.
      - Leader: Montezuma
      - OpponentID: 2
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early world.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-world science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Combined Arms: Allows you to build the Tank, a fast and deadly armor Unit.
        Completing it would unlock: Radar
      - Penicillin: All Workers receive the Fallout Reduction Promotion, which reduces the amount of damage they take while scrubbing Fallout. Allows you to build the Marine, a Unit that specializes in amphibious assaults. Also allows you to build the Medical Lab, a building which speeds the growth of your Cities.
        Completing it would unlock: Electronics
      - Computers: Grants an additional Trade Route. Allows you to build the Carrier, a naval unit capable of carrying aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Telecommunications, Satellites
      
      ## Policies
      - Air Supremacy (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Air Supremacy: Receive a free Airport in every City. +25% Production when building Aircraft. Can build Zeroes.
      - Co-Prosperity Sphere (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Co-Prosperity Sphere: Bullying City-States no longer reduces Influence, revokes Quests, or cancels Pledges of Protection. Doing so decreases the Influence of all other Civs with the bullied City-State by 10%, and increases your Influence by 10%.
      - Cult of Personality (Level 3 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Cult of Personality: +50% Tourism to civilizations fighting a common enemy. 50% of your highest Warscore counts as a Tourism Modifier with all Civilizations. Free Great Person of your choice.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - New World Order (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): New World Order: -2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all Cities. Police Stations and Constabularies provide +3 Culture and +5 Production, and are constructed 100% more quickly.
      - Iron Fist (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Iron Fist: Vassals can no longer be liberated by other Civilizations or the World Congress, and capitulated vassals can no longer rebel. Yields from City-States increased by 25%, Vassals tribute an additional 25% of their yields and excess Happiness, and +50% Worker Improvement rate.
      - Regimental System (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * Earn Great Generals and Great Admirals 33% faster.
        - * Great Generals and Great Admirals gain the Regimental Traditions Promotion (+10% Leadership Combat Bonus and +1 Tile Radius).
        - * +2 additional copies of the unique Luxury Resources from expending Great Admirals for their Voyage of Discovery ability.
        - * Military Units can be upgraded in territory owned by Vassals and friendly City-States.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Fiefdoms (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness for every 10 Military Units in Empire.
        - * +15% Production and +100% Border Growth during "We Love the King Day."
      - Organized Religion (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +25% Pressure from owned Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +1 Delegate in the World Congress for every 10 Cities following your Primary Religion.
        - * +2 Culture from Shrines and Temples.
      - Divine Right (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +33% Yields from Internal Trade Routes.
        - * Completing an Internal Trade Route triggers a Historic Event as if completing an International Trade Route.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from Boredom in all Cities.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Scientific Revolution (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Can construct Observatories (+6 Science, +1 Science from nearby Mountains, 2 Scientist Slot).
        - * +2 Science from Jungle and Snow.
      - Shadow Networks (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 25% of Science generated by Allied City-States.
        - * +3% Culture in Capital for every 100 Spy Points ever accumulated (up to 30%).
        - * +3 Science from Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - * +1 Science from Specialists.
      - Free Trade (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * Earn Great Merchants 50% faster.
        - * +5 Gold from International Trade Routes.
        - * -2 Unhappiness from Poverty in all Cities.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Enlightenment (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Receive 1 Free Technology.
        - * +1 Happiness from Universities.
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Progress (New Branch):
        - Progress is best for civilizations which desire robust infrastructure and scientific advancement.
        - Adopting Progress grants:
        - * Receive 20 Science when a Citizen is born in your Capital and 15 Science for every Citizen already in your Capital, both scaling with Era.
        - * Receive 15 Culture when a Technology is researched, scaling with Era, and for every Technology already unlocked (not scaling with Era).
        - Each Progress policy unlocked grants:
        - * Gain an additional 15 Culture when a Technology is researched, scaling with Era.
        - Adopting all policies in Progress grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Forbidden Palace.
        - * Receive 25 Gold when a Citizen is born in any city, scaling with Era.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Writers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

          - text: Let me analyze the current situation carefully.
            
            **Key Events from Turn 420-421:**
            
            1. **Manhattan Project**: Tenochtitlan was building it with 1 turn left. It should now be complete! This means we can now build atomic bombs.
            
            2. **Babylon built Nuclear Power Plant in Babylon** and **Military Base in Borsippa** - they're fortifying their core cities.
            
            3. **Babylon has XCOM Squads** - they've reached very advanced tech (Information era units).
            
            4. **Babylon built a Carrier** - they're expanding their naval air capability.
            
            5. **Lost a Destroyer to Babylon** - naval attrition continues.
            
            6. **Killed a Babylon Fleet Destroyer** - some success at sea.
            
            7. **Bunkeya flipped to Japan** as ally - we lost influence there.
            
            8. **Rio de Janeiro** - our friendship dropped from 58 to 28.
            
            9. **Our spy in Borsippa** - Agent Nahuatl arrived but spy state shows 0 then 1 (traveling then gathering intelligence).
            
            10. **Multiple city-state elections** - mixed results. Japan's spies are very active.
            
            11. **Babylon's Lyon is still razing** (8 turns -> now down to pop 10 from 11).
            
            12. **Military situation**: We're attacking Nippur (damage 139 to city), and we have forces near Borsippa. The Borsippa zone is "Even" dominance - we need to push there.
            
            13. **Buffalo Creek** built Arsenal, Teotihuacan built Factory, Tlaxcala built Research Lab.
            
            14. **Veligrad** - our influence dropped significantly (election failure, -3000 diminish amount).
            
            15. **America** sent ambassadors/inquisitors to Hanoi area - they're competing for city-state influence.
            
            **Current Strategic Assessment:**
            
            - **Manhattan Project complete** (or completing this turn) - we can now build atomic bombs!
            - **Nuclear Fission** researching in 11 turns - we need this for uranium-based weapons
            - **Babylon is in Information Era** with XCOM Squads - they're technologically ahead
            - **War score against Babylon: 12%** - slow progress
            - **Babylon has 2 Uranium, we have 0 Uranium** - this is a CRITICAL problem for nuclear weapons
            - Wait - we need Uranium to build nukes. We have 0 Uranium! Babylon has 2. Ottomans have 5.
            - Our Aluminum is 24 - good for aircraft but not nukes.
            
            **Uranium Problem**: We have 0 Uranium. We need it for atomic bombs and nuclear power. The Ottomans have 5 Uranium - we should try to trade for it. Or we need to find and improve Uranium resources.
            
            Looking at our resources: Uranium: 0. This is a major issue. Without uranium, we can't build nuclear weapons even with the Manhattan Project complete.
            
            Wait - looking at the tile improvements, I see Ottomans have a Uranium quarry at 55,23 near Grand River. We have a Uranium improvement being built at 57,30 (our territory - "Remove Jungle" on Plains Land 57,30 with Uranium resource). That could give us uranium!
            
            Actually, let me recheck. In the events, a worker was removing jungle at "Plains Land 57,30: Owner: The Aztecs, IsRiver: true, Resource: Uranium, Improvement: Lumber Mill, Feature: Jungle". So we DO have uranium in our territory! Once the jungle is removed and it's properly improved, we should get uranium.
            
            But the improvement shown is "Lumber Mill" - the jungle removal was completed (event 158). After jungle removal, the tile would need a mine or proper improvement to access the uranium. Let me check if there's more...
            
            Actually, looking at Brantford city data, it doesn't list Uranium in its resources. But the tile at 57,30 has Uranium. The worker just removed the jungle - they might need to build a mine next. We need to prioritize this!
            
            Also checking the uranium situation more carefully - we need to see if we have any uranium resources being worked. The tile 57,30 near Brantford has Uranium but currently shows "Lumber Mill" improvement and was jungle. Now jungle removed, needs a mine to access uranium.
            
            **Strategic Priorities:**
            
            1. **Get Uranium online ASAP** - Need to mine our uranium at 57,30 to build nukes
            2. **Continue pressure on Borsippa** - Our most promising target (even dominance, 240 zone value)
            3. **Push Nippur** - Taking heavy damage (139 damage this turn)
            4. **Research Computers** - For carriers (needed for naval air power) and extra trade route
            5. **Air Supremacy policy** when available (26 turns) - free airports, +25% aircraft production
            6. **Build atomic bombs** once we have uranium
            
            **Technology**: Nuclear Fission is researching (11 turns). After that, we need Nuclear Fusion for thermonuclear weapons and more advanced tech. But Computers gives us carriers and an extra trade route - very valuable.
            
            Looking at the tech options:
            - Combined Arms: Tanks (good for ground offense)
            - Penicillin: Marines, Medical Labs
            - Computers: Carrier, extra trade route, unlocks Telecommunications and Satellites
            
            I think after Nuclear Fission, we should go for Computers for the carrier capability and extra trade route. Or Combined Arms for tanks.
            
            Actually, wait - we need to think about what unlocks nuclear weapons delivery. Atomic bombs can be delivered by bombers or carriers. We need to get nuclear capability operational ASAP.
            
            **Policy**: Air Supremacy is set (26 turns until next policy). That's fine - we need the free airports and aircraft production bonus.
            
            **Relationships**: 
            - Bunkeya flipped to Japan as ally - annoying but expected
            - Veligrad influence dropped due to election failure
            - Hanoi remains our friend with 336 influence
            
            Let me make my decisions:
            
            1. **Flavors/Strategy**: Maintain conquest focus. Manhattan Project complete means we need uranium mining ASAP. Increase emphasis on getting uranium operational and building nukes. Keep air and anti-air priorities high.
            
            2. **Research**: After Nuclear Fission completes, set Computers for carrier capability and extra trade route. Actually, Combined Arms might be better for Tank production for ground assault. Let me think... We already have landships and the Ottomans just built a Tank. Tanks (75 strength) are powerful. But carriers are essential for nuclear delivery at sea. Actually, atomic bombs are delivered by bombers, and we have heavy bombers already. The issue is getting them close enough. We need carriers or forward air bases.
            
            Wait - we have airbases in our cities. We can rebase bombers closer. Let me check if we have bombers near Babylon... Looking at military zones:
            - Zone 3285 (Borsippa): No Aztec bombers listed
            - Zone 2315 (Nippur): No Aztec bombers, just riflemen
            - Zone 10811 (Xochicalco): Has 1 bomber
            - Zone 10234 (Brantford): Has 1 bomber
            
            We have bombers but they're far from Babylon's core. We need to rebase or build carriers.
            
            I'll set research to Computers after Nuclear Fission - the carrier and extra trade route are both critical.
            
            3. **Relationship updates**: 
            - Bunkeya: Now Japan's ally. We should adjust our relationship downward.
            - Veligrad: Influence dropped significantly. Need to reassess.
            
            4. **Persona**: May need minor adjustments but current settings are aggressive enough.
            
            Let me think about what changes to make:
            
            **Flavors**: 
            - Keep Conquest grand strategy
            - Uranium access is critical - but there's no "Uranium" flavor. We need TileImprovement and Production to ensure workers mine the uranium.
            - Actually, we should prioritize getting the uranium mined. I should increase TileImprovement temporarily.
            - Science remains high to reach Nuclear Fission faster
            - Keep military flavors high
            
            Let me be strategic about what to adjust:
            
            Current key issues:
            1. We need to mine uranium at 57,30 (worker just removed jungle there)
            2. Manhattan Project should be complete - need to build atomic bombs in cities
            3. Nippur is taking heavy damage - continue the push
            4. Borsippa is the high-value target (240 zone value)
            5. Babylon has XCOM Squads and Fleet Destroyers - they're getting advanced units
            
            I think the strategy should remain mostly the same with slight adjustments:
            
            - Increase TileImprovement to ensure uranium gets mined
            - Keep all military priorities high
            - Maintain science focus
            
            Let me finalize my decisions and make the tool calls.
            
            For the relationship with Bunkeya - since Japan took the alliance and we lost our pledge (broken), I should adjust. Bunkeya is now Japan's ally. We should reduce our positive relationship since it's no longer productive.
            
            Actually, looking more carefully - our relationship with Bunkeya was 20/10 (low positive). With Japan taking ally, we can't compete. Let me keep it as is for now since it's already low.
            
            Veligrad - Japan took alliance (764 influence vs our 214). Our relationship was 100/85 which is too high given we can't compete. Let me reduce it.
            
            Let me set up my tool calls:
            
            1. **set-flavors**: Maintain conquest, adjust some flavors
            2. **set-research**: Set Computers as next research after Nuclear Fission
            3. **set-relationship**: Adjust Veligrad (now Japan's ally, can't compete)
            4. **set-relationship**: Maybe adjust Bunkeya slightly
            
            Actually, I need to be careful. Let me focus on the most impactful changes.
            
            For flavors, I want to:
            - Keep Conquest grand strategy
            - Increase TileImprovement from 30 to 50 to ensure uranium mining gets done
            - Keep most military flavors as is
            - Maybe slightly increase Growth for population
            - Maintain Science at 95
            
            For research, after Nuclear Fission (11 turns), I'll set Computers.
            
            For relationships:
            - Veligrad: Japan is now ally at 764 influence vs our 214. Reduce from 100/85 to 50/40 since we can't realistically compete for alliance.
            - Keep everything else stable.
            
            Let me also check - do we need to worry about Japan's cultural victory? Japan has 2 Influential civs (Celts and us) and is close to getting more. Their tourism is 1038/turn with 3819 culture/turn. They need 6 influential for cultural victory. Currently at 2. France is 4 turns away, Ottomans 5 turns away - that would bring them to 4. Then they need 2 more. This is concerning.
            
            Actually wait - Japan is already Influential over The Aztecs (101%). And Celts (147%). France is 4 turns away, Ottomans is 5 turns. That would be 4 Influential. Then they need 2 more (America at 55 turns, Babylon at 18 turns). So Japan could have 6 Influential within ~55 turns if trends continue.
            
            But cultural victory also requires: an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets AND the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder. Japan has Autocracy with 4 tenets - they need 2 more Tier 3 tenets and the wonder. That takes significant time.
            
            Still, Japan is the biggest long-term threat after Babylon. For now, Babylon's science victory is more immediate.
            
            Let me finalize my decisions and make calls.

        - #1:
          - type: text
          - text: Looking at the situation after Turn 421, several critical developments:
            
            1. **Manhattan Project** should be completing in Tenochtitlan (was 1 turn away) - we can now build atomic bombs!
